Compute W array length Lw = 2n-1. In logsum, c_vec computed by compute_c5_vec (not dx? weights for objective scaled? dx factor can be omitted if consistent? Gradient of smooth objective with dx included? We can include dx in c or not. Objective value smooth = m + logsum/alpha + penalty. If m from values with dx, weights w correspond to exp(alpha(c-m)) where c includes dx. Gradient of m? Actually logsumexp objective = (1/alpha) log ∑ exp(alpha c_i) approximates max c_i. Gradient = ∑ w_i grad c_i. c_i = f_i * dx. So weights w_i for c_i. Derivative wrt h of c_i is dx * derivative f_i. We can compute base grad for f then multiply dx. In our formula grad_f = conv...; gradient = dx*grad_f. We'll include dx.

So smooth_grad(x,n,alpha,mu):
  c = compute_c5_vec(x,n)  # includes dx
  m = c.max()
  z = alpha*(c-m); mx=z.max(); w=exp(z-mx); w/=w.sum()
  A = w (length 2n-1)
  grad_f = convolution_terms(A, x, n)
  grad = dx * grad_f + 2*mu*(x.sum()-n/2)*ones
  return grad

Need implement convolution_terms efficiently. We can use np.fft each call. But for n small (≤300), direct might be simpler and faster. We can implement direct vectorized using FFT? Let's design function weighted_grad_fast(w, x, n):
  L=2*n-1
  # if n <= 400 maybe use direct cumulative? Could use loops over w nonzero threshold; but w dense. We can use np.add.reduce? Maybe direct using for k? Let's keep FFT for n>150, direct for n<=150.
Direct loop over lags with significant w:
  grad = zeros(n); dx=2/n
  for m, wm in enumerate(w):
    if abs(wm) < eps: continue
    k = m - (n-1)
    if k>=0:
      nv=n-k
      grad[0:nv] += wm*(1-x[k:n])
      grad[k:n] += wm*(-x[0:nv])
    else:
      kk=-k
      nv=n-kk
      grad[kk:n] += wm*(1-x[0:nv])
      grad[0:nv] += wm*(-x[kk:n])
  return grad
This is parent. For n=500, 999 loops, each vector length ~500 => 500k operations plus Python loop 1000. Might be ~0.01s? Maybe okay for a few hundred gradients, not thousands. For low n, fine.

FFT version:
  def conv_slice(A, X, n):
    Lw = 2*n -1
    # len conv = Lw + n -1 = 3n-2
    N = 1 << ((3*n-2).bit_length())  # power of two maybe
    fa = np.fft.rfft(A, N)
    fx = np.fft.rfft(X, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa*fx, N)[:3*n-2]
    return c[n-1:2*n-1]
  A = np.asarray(w)
  X1 = 1-x
  term1 = conv_slice(A[::-1], X1, n)
  term2 = conv_slice(A, x, n)
  return term1 - term2

  S = w.sum()
  C1 = conv(flip(A), x)[n-1:n-1+n]

Smooth objective and gradient:
We can implement smooth_obj and smooth_grad as functions. For gradient direct: weighted_grad_direct(w,h,n):
  grad = zeros(n)
  active = np.where(np.abs(w)>1e-12)[0]
  for m in active:
    k = m - (n-1)
    wm = w[m]
    if k>=0:
      nv = n-k
      grad[:nv] += wm*(1.0-h[k:])
      grad[k:] += -wm*h[:nv]
    else:
      kk=-k
      nv=n-kk
      grad[kk:] += wm*(1.0-h[:nv])
      grad[:nv] += -wm*h[kk:]
  return grad * (2/n)

def resample_by_mass(h, m):
  n=len(h); if m==n return h.copy()
  dx_old=2.0/n
  c = np.concatenate(([0.0], np.cumsum(h)*dx_old))
  x = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, m+1)
  # np.interp needs increasing xp; c length n+1 for endpoints 0..2. For exact endpoints okay.
  cum_new = np.interp(x, np.linspace(0,2,n+1), c, left=0, right=1) ? Actually c corresponds to x_old_end = i*2/n for i=0..n. Use xp = np.linspace(0,2,n+1). Good.
  mass = cum_new[1:] - cum_new[:-1]
  h_new = mass / (2.0/m)
  # project to bounds and exact sum
  return project_feasible(h_new, m)
This preserves integral in L1 sense.

Need implement line_search_pair efficiently:
def pair_line_search(h, n, i, j, c_vec_base):
  # transfer t: h[i] += t, h[j] -= t. t in [0, min(1-h[i], h[j])]. If i==j return (0,base max)
  L_i = deriv_lag_vector(h,n,i) # no dx
  L_j = deriv_lag_vector(h,n,j)
  diff = L_i - L_j
  dx=2/n
  m_pair = n-1 + abs(i-j)
  # c(t)=c0 + dx*t*diff + dx*t^2 at m_pair
  # find min max
  # We can evaluate via grid.
  t_max = min(1-h[i], h[j]); if t_max<1e-6 return 0, c0max
  best_t=0; best_c=c0max
  # Stage 1: np.linspace(0,t_max,41)
  for points:
    vals = c_vec_base + dx*t*diff

def deriv_lag_vector(h,n,p):
  L = np.zeros(2*n-1)
  start1 = n-1-p
  L[start1:start1+n] += 1.0 - h
  start2 = p
  L[p:p+n] -= h
  return L

project_feasible exact:
```
def project_feasible(h, n):
    h = np.array(h, dtype=float).clip(0.0,1.0)
    target = 0.5*n
    for _ in range(200):
        s = h.sum()
        diff = target - s
        if abs(diff) <= 1e-13: break
        if diff > 0:
            room = 1.0 - h
            tot = room.sum()
            if tot <= 1e-15: break
            h += (diff/tot)*room
        else:
            tot = s
            if tot <= 1e-15: break
            h += (diff/tot)*h
    h = np.clip(h,0,1)
    # final adjust
    s = h.sum(); diff=target-s
    if abs(diff)>1e-12:
       if diff>0:
         idx = np.flatnonzero(h<1.0); if idx.size:
           room=1-h[idx]; order=np.argsort(-room); add=min(diff,room[order[0]]?) distribute.
       else similar
    return h
```
Simpler final: Use proportional again; already. To force sum exactly, use `h[h.argmax? ]`? If diff small, just adjust an interior element:
```
if abs(h.sum()-target)>1e-12:
   interior = np.where((h>1e-12)&(h<1-1e-12))[0]
   if len>0 and abs(diff) <= max(h[interior],1-h[interior]): h[interior[0]] += diff
   else: h = project again
```
But not critical. Use parent loop.

compute_c5_vec: For performance, if n small maybe dtype float.
```
def compute_c5_vec(h,n):
    dx=2.0/n
    return np.correlate(h, 1.0-h, mode='full')*dx
```
`h` feasible. `1-h` computed.

resample_by_mass:
```
def resample_by_mass(h, m):
    n=len(h)
    if m==n: return h.copy()
    if m<=0: return np.array([])
    xp = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n+1)
    cp = np.concatenate(([0.0], np.cumsum(h)*(2.0/n)))
    cp = np.clip(cp,0,1?) Actually total 1.
    x = np.linspace(0.0,2.0,m+1)
    cnew = np.interp(x, xp, cp, left=0.0, right=1.0)
    mass = np.diff(cnew)
    hnew = mass*(m/2.0)  # dx=2/m, mass/dx
    return project_feasible(hnew, m)
```
Since cp total 1, right=1. For x endpoint 2, interp cp[-1]. Good.

def smooth_obj(x,n,alpha,mu):
    x = np.clip(x,0,1)
    c=compute_c5_vec(x,n)
    m=c.max()

    mx=sh.max()
    if mx>500: val=m + (mx+np.log(np.sum(np.exp(sh-mx))))/alpha
    else: val=m+np.log(np.sum(np.exp(sh)))/alpha

```

def smooth_grad(x,n,alpha,mu):
    x=np.clip(x,0,1)
    c=compute_c5_vec(x,n)
    m=c.max()
    sh=alpha*(c-m)
    mx=sh.max()
    w=np.exp(sh-mx); w/=w.sum()
    # threshold
    if n <= 2000:
       wf = w.copy(); wf[np.abs(wf)<1e-10]=0
       grad=weighted_grad_direct(wf,x,n)
    else: ...
    grad += 2.0*mu*(x.sum()-0.5*n)
    return grad
```

lbfgs_optimize:

def lbfgs_optimize(h,n,deadline,configs=None):
    h=project_feasible(h,n)
    best_h=h.copy(); best_c=compute_c5(h,n)
    bounds=[(0.0,1.0)]*n
    if configs is None:
      if n<=64: configs=[(20,100),(100,1000),(500,10000),(2000,100000)]
      elif n<=256: [(50,100),(200,1000),(1000,10000),(4000,100000)]
      else: [(500,100),(2000,1000),(8000,10000)]
    for alpha,mu in configs:
       if time.time()>deadline-1: break
       try:
         options maxiter based on n: n<=64 3000; <=256 2000; else 1000
         res=minimize(smooth_obj,h,jac=smooth_grad,args=(n,alpha,mu),method='L-BFGS-B',bounds=bounds,options={'maxiter':maxiter,'ftol':1e-16,'gtol':1e-14,'maxfun':max(maxiter*20,2000)})
         h_new=project_feasible(res.x,n)
         c_new=compute_c5(h_new,n)
         if c_new<best_c-1e-15:
            best_c=c_new; best_h=h_new.copy(); h=h_new
       except Exception: pass
    return best_h,best_c
```

def sqp_optimize(h,n,deadline,max_iter=None,rho_init=0.05,max_active=None):
  h=project_feasible(h,n); best...
  if max_iter is None: max_iter = 80 if n>800 else 200
  if max_active is None: max_active = 60 if n>500 else 100
  rho=rho_init; no=0
  for it in range(max_iter):
    if time.time()>deadline: break
    c=compute_c5_vec(h,n); maxv=c.max()
    # active: all >= max - tol
    tol = max(1e-10, 1e-5*maxv) maybe.
    active = np.where(c >= maxv - tol)[0]
    if len(active)>max_active: active=active[np.argsort(-c[active])[:max_active]]
    # build LP
    grads=[]; cvals=[]
    for idx in active:
       g=single_shift_grad(h,n,int(idx)); grads.append(g); cvals.append(c[idx])
    A_ub = np.zeros((len(active), n+1)); for j: A_ub[j,:n]=grads[j]; A_ub[j,n]=-1; b_ub=-cvals
    A_eq = np.zeros((1,n+1)); A_eq[0,:n]=1
    bounds=[]
    for i in range(n):
       lo=max(-rho, -h[i]); up=min(rho, 1-h[i]); bounds.append((lo,up))
    bounds.append((0.0, None))
    try: res=linprog(...)
    except: res=None
    if not res.success: rho*=0.5; no++; continue
    delta=res.x[:n]
    # check step
    if max(abs(delta)) < 1e-12: break
    improved=False
    for factor in [1.0,0.5,0.25,0.125]:
       h_try=project_feasible(h+factor*delta,n)
       c_try=compute_c5(h_try,n)
       if c_try < best_c - 1e-15:
          best_c=c_try; best_h=h_try.copy(); h=h_try; improved=True; break
    if improved:
       no=0; rho=min(0.12, rho*1.25)
    else:
       no+=1; rho*=0.5
       if rho<1e-8 or no>25: break
  return best_h,best_c
```
Potential issue: Objective t minimized may choose delta with c_try not lower than best (which includes maybe inactive constraints increased). Backtrack handles.
`single_shift_grad` includes dx. Good.
Use `method='highs'`. If linprog fails due precision, catch.

Pair descent details:
```
def deriv_lag_vector(h,n,p):
    L = np.zeros(2*n-1)
    L[n-1-p:2*n-1-p] += 1.0 - h
    L[p:p+n] -= h[::-1]
    return L

def pair_line_search(h,n,i,j,c_base):
    if i==j: return 0.0,float(c_base.max())
    hi=1.0-h[i]; hj=h[j]
    tmax=min(hi,hj)
    if tmax < 1e-7: return 0.0,float(c_base.max())
    Li=deriv_lag_vector(h,n,i)
    Lj=deriv_lag_vector(h,n,j)
    diff=Li-Lj
    dx=2.0/n; mp=n-1+abs(i-j); base=c_base
    best_t=0.0; best_c=float(base.max())
    def eval_t(t): # cannot closure! Need helper top-level? We can implement loops inline, not nested.
```
No closures: cannot define inner eval_t. We can write a helper `pair_eval_max(base,diff,mp,dx,t)` top-level. But creating helper okay. Or use vectorized grid with for loops and compute values.
Define top-level:
```
def pair_candidate_max(base, diff, mp, dx, t):
    if t == 0.0:
       return float(base.max())
    vals = base + dx*t*diff
    vals[mp] += dx*t*t
    return float(vals.max())
```
This allocates each call. For grid, okay. To reduce allocation, maybe pass tmp array? Top-level can't easily. Fine.

pair_descent:
```
def pair_descent(h,n,deadline,max_iter=200,max_pairs=200, active_tol=1e-4, search_n_max=2000):
    h=project_feasible(h,n); best=...
    c=compute_c5_vec(h,n)
    no_improve=0
    iter=0
    while iter<max_iter and time.time()<deadline:
        iter+=1
        maxv=c.max()
        # active top for gradient
        # weights uniform over active near max, or top K
        tol = max(1e-8, active_tol*maxv?) maybe use 1e-4.
        act = np.where(c >= maxv - max(1e-8, 0.001*maxv))[0]
        if act.size==0: act=np.array([np.argmax(c)])
        if act.size > 30: act=act[np.argsort(-c[act])[:30]]
        # compute gradient = mean single_shift_grad over active
        g=np.zeros(n)
        for idx in act: g += single_shift_grad(h,n,int(idx))
        g /= act.size
        # candidate indices: if h interior
        interior = np.where((h > 1e-4) & (h < 1-1e-4))[0]
        if interior.size <2: break
        # sort: low g (increase), high g (decrease)
        order=np.argsort(g[interior])
        low = interior[order[:20]]
        high = interior[order[-20:]]
        pairs=[]
        for i in low:
           for j in high:
              if i!=j: pairs.append((int(i),int(j)))
        # optionally random pairs 50
        np.random.shuffle(pairs)
        pairs=pairs[:max_pairs]
        improved=False
        for i,j in pairs:
            if time.time()>deadline: break
            # only if direction beneficial roughly
            if g[i] < g[j] - 1e-6:
                t,bc=pair_line_search(h,n,i,j,c)
                if bc < best - 1e-13:
                    h[i]+=t; h[j]-=t; h=project_feasible(h,n); best=float(c.max())? recompute c? We should recompute c=compute_c5_vec(h,n); best=c.max(); improved=True; break? We can break to update all changes; continue loops maybe break for this round.
            # if g[j] < g[i], reverse
            elif g[j] < g[i] - 1e-6:
                t,bc=pair_line_search(h,n,j,i,c) # increase j decrease i
                ...
        if improved:
           no_improve=0
        else:
           no_improve+=1
           if no_improve>5: break
           # random kick: pick pair and large t? To escape
           if n<=500:
              while random...
              h=project_feasible
              c=compute...
        else recompute c if improved: c=compute_c5_vec(h,n)
    return h,best
```

def binary_hill_climb(h,n,deadline,max_evals=200000, allow_worse=False):

      # maybe limit pairs if n large: shuffled

        for o in ones:

              cur[z]=0; cur[o]=1

      passes+=1
      if not improved: break
   # random annealing from best
   ...

make_binary_from_h:
```
def make_binary(h,n):
    h=project_feasible(h,n)
    if n%2==0:
       k=n//2
       idx=np.argsort(h)[::-1]
       b=np.zeros(n); b[idx[:k]]=1
    else:
       k=(n-1)//2; idx=...; b[idx[:k]]=1; b[idx[k]]=0.5
    return b
```

def de_lowdim(n, deadline, candidates):
   pop=[]; # add candidates projected, then random fill to popsize
   popsize = max(16, min(60, 4*n))
   vals=[]
   for cand in candidates: pop.append(project_feasible(cand,n)); vals.append(compute_c5)
   while len(pop)<popsize: pop.append(rand_feasible(n)); vals.append(...)
   best idx = argmin(vals)
   best_c=vals[best]; best_h=pop[best]
   F=0.85; CR=0.7; evals=0
   max_evals=time-dependent: if n<=32 30000; n<=64 15000; else 6000
   while time<deadline and evals<max_evals:
      for i in range(len(pop)):
         if time > deadline or evals>=max_evals: break
         # choose r different
         choices = [x for x in range(len(pop)) if x!=i]
         r1,r2,r3 = np.random.choice(choices, size=3, replace=False)
         mut = pop[r1] + F*(pop[r2]-pop[r3])
         child = pop[i].copy()
         mask = np.random.rand(n) < CR
         if not mask.any(): mask[np.random.randint(n)]=True
         child[mask] = mut[mask]
         # clip/project
         child = np.clip(child,0,1); child=project_feasible(child,n)
         c=compute_c5(child,n); evals+=1
         if c < vals[i]: pop[i]=child; vals[i]=c
         if c < vals[best? pop changes difficult with best index. We can maintain best_c separately.
         if c < best_c: best_c=c; best_h=child.copy()
      # if no best improvement for many generations, maybe increase F? Use stagnation.
   # local polish top 3
   # select top 3 unique by vals
   order=np.argsort(vals)
   for k in order[:3]:
      h=pop[k].copy()
      if time left: hc=lbfgs small? maybe pair_descent/sqp
   return best_h,best_c
```

Let's write de_lowdim carefully no closures:
```
def de_lowdim(n, deadline, candidates, max_evals=10000, seed=1):
    np.random.seed(seed)
    pop=[]; vals=[]
    # add candidates
    for cand in candidates:
       h=project_feasible(np.asarray(cand,dtype=float), n)
       pop.append(h); vals.append(compute_c5(h,n))
    popsize=max(16, min(60, 4*n))
    while len(pop)<popsize:
       h=rand_feasible(n)
       pop.append(h); vals.append(compute_c5(h,n))
    best_h=pop[int(np.argmin(vals))].copy(); best_c=min(vals)
    stagn=0
    F=0.85; CR=0.7
    evals=0
    while time.time()<deadline and evals<max_evals:
       improved_gen=False
       P=len(pop)
       for i in range(P):
          if time.time()<0.05 or evals>=max_evals: break
          # choose distinct
          choices = [x for x in range(P) if x != i]
          if len(choices)<3: break
          r1,r2,r3 = [int(x) for x in np.random.choice(choices, size=3, replace=False)]
          mut = pop[r1] + F*(pop[r2]-pop[r3])
          child = pop[i].copy()
          mask = np.random.rand(n) < CR
          if not mask.any():
             mask[int(np.random.randint(n))] = True
          child[mask] = mut[mask]
          # maybe add small noise to all for diversity
          # child += 0.01*np.random.randn(n) if stagn>5?
          child = np.clip(child,0.0,1.0)
          child = project_feasible(child,n)
          c=compute_c5(child,n); evals+=1
          if c < vals[i]:
             pop[i]=child; vals[i]=c; improved_gen=True
          if c < best_c:
             best_c=c; best_h=child.copy(); improved_gen=True
       if improved_gen: stagn=0
       else:
          stagn+=1
          if stagn>5:
             # replace worst with best + noise
             worst=int(np.argmax(vals))
             h=best_h.copy()
             noise=np.random.randn(n)
             h=np.clip(h+0.1*noise,0,1); h=project_feasible(h,n)
             c=compute_c5(h,n); evals+=1
             pop[worst]=h; vals[worst]=c
             if c<best_c: best_h=h.copy(); best_c=c
             stagn=0
    # local polish top 3
    order=np.argsort(vals)
    for t in range(min(3,len(order))):
       idx=int(order[t])
       h=pop[idx].copy()
       if time.time()<deadline-2:
          h,c=pair_descent(h,n,deadline,max_iter=20,max_pairs=50)
          if c<best_c: best_c=c; best_h=h.copy()
    return best_h,best_c
```
Time check `time.time()<0.05` bug; should `time.time() > deadline - 0.2`. Use proper.
`np.random.choice` with list returns array. `choices` list creation each i P~30, okay. For speed, use precomputed? fine.
Could set max_evals by n in run.

def binary_hill_climb(h, n, deadline, max_evals=50000):
    h=make_binary(h,n).astype(float)
    cur=h.copy(); cur_c=compute_c5(cur,n)
    best=cur.copy(); best_c=cur_c
    passes=0; evals=0
    while time.time()<deadline and passes<50 and evals<max_evals:
       improved=False
       zeros=np.where(cur<0.5)[0].tolist(); ones=np.where(cur>0.5)[0].tolist()
       # for large n, random sample pairs to avoid O(n^2)
       if n* n > 8000: # n>90
          pairs = [(int(np.random.randint(len(zeros))), int(np.random.randint(len(ones)))) for _ in range(500)]
       else:
          pairs = [(a,b) for a in zeros for b in ones]
       # maybe shuffle if many
       if len(pairs)>2000:
          pairs = [pairs[i] for i in np.random.choice(len(pairs), size=2000, replace=False)]
       for za, oa in pairs:
          z=zeros[za]; o=ones[oa]
          if z==o: continue
          if cur[z] != 1 -? Actually binary but maybe 0.5; use cur[z]=0; cur[o]=1? For even: swap: cur[z]=1-cur[z]? If cur[z]=0, cur[o]=1. For safety:
          oldz=cur[z]; oldo=cur[o]
          cur[z]=1.0-oldz? no if 0.5? We only use zeros<0.5 and ones>0.5; set cur[z]=1.0; cur[o]=0.0
          c=compute_c5(cur,n); evals+=1
          if c < cur_c - 1e-15:
             cur_c=c; improved=True
             if c<best_c: best=cur.copy(); best_c=c
             break
          else:
             cur[z]=oldz; cur[o]=oldo
          if evals>=max_evals: break
       passes+=1
       if not improved:
          # random kick: try 100 random swaps accept if <=cur_c? maybe
          kick=0
          for _ in range(100):
             z=int(np.random.randint(len(zeros))); o=int(np.random.randint(len(ones)))
             oldz=cur[z]; oldo=cur[o]; cur[z]=1.0; cur[o]=0.0
             c=compute_c5(cur,n); evals+=1
             if c <= cur_c + 1e-12:
                cur_c=c; improved=True
                if c<best_c: best=cur.copy(); best_c=c
                kick=1
             else: cur[z]=oldz; cur[o]=oldo
             if evals>=max_evals: break
          if not kick: break
    return best,best_c

`weighted_grad_direct` loops all active; used in pair_descent and smooth_grad. It multiplies dx. Let's implement with threshold.
```
def weighted_grad_direct(w, h, n):
    grad=np.zeros(n)
    # threshold
    thresh=1e-12
    for m, wm in enumerate(w):
       if abs(wm) <= thresh: continue
       k=m-(n-1)
       if k>=0:
          nv=n-k
          if nv>0:
             grad[:nv] += wm*(1-h[k:])
             grad[k:] += -wm*h[:nv]
       else:
          kk=-k; nv=n-kk
          if nv>0:
             grad[kk:] += wm*(1-h[:nv])
             grad[:nv] += -wm*h[kk:]
    grad *= 2.0/n
    return grad
```
Check for k=0: nv=n; first grad[:n] += wm*(1-h[:n]); second grad[0:] += -wm*h[:n] => 1-2h, good.

In smooth_grad, `w` sum1. Use `if n <= 800 or active_count < 4*n`? Actually weighted_grad_direct cost O(num_active*n). For n=2000 and dense active 3999 ->8M ops per grad, maybe 0.1s. L-BFGS 500 grads 50s. Maybe okay? But with Python loops 4000 -> 0.04s overhead, 500=20s plus C ops. Could be okay. We can set FFT for n>1200 later. For simplicity direct. But L-BFGS for n=1000 with alpha small dense may be slow; we can start alpha high to reduce active. Or in smooth_grad, threshold w<1e-8 to reduce. For alpha=1000, weights concentrated. For alpha=10, active dense but n small. Good.

SLSQP polish top-level:
```
def slqp_obj(x,n,alpha):
   x=np.clip(x,0,1)
   c=compute_c5_vec(x,n)
   m=c.max()
   sh=alpha*(c-m); mx=sh.max()
   return float(m + (mx+np.log(np.sum(np.exp(sh-mx))))/alpha)

def slqp_eq(x,n):
   return float(x.sum()-0.5*n)
def slqp_eq_jac(x,n):
   return np.ones(n)
def slqp_polish(h,n,deadline,alpha=50000,maxiter=1000):
   if time.time()>deadline-5: return h, compute_c5(h,n)
   bounds=[(0,1)]*n
   cons=[{'type':'eq','fun':slqp_eq,'jac':slqp_eq_jac,'args':(n,)}]
   try:
      res=minimize(slqp_obj,h,method='SLSQP',bounds=bounds,constraints=cons,options={'maxiter':maxiter,'ftol':1e-15})
      h_new=project_feasible(res.x,n); c_new=compute_c5(h_new,n); return h_new,c_new
   except: return h, compute_c5(h,n)
```
But `slqp_obj` lacks penalty for sum; constraint handles. SLSQP may be slow for n>200. Use for low/medium final maybe. If constraint infeasible? h feasible. Good.

`project_feasible` after clipping can make sum not exact due to final clip. Our proportional update should keep within bounds. Let's write robust:
```
def project_feasible(h,n):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float).copy()
    h = np.clip(h,0.0,1.0)
    target=0.5*n
    for _ in range(100):
        s=h.sum(); diff=target-s
        if abs(diff)<=1e-14: break
        if diff>0:
            room=1-h; tot=room.sum()
            if tot<=1e-14: break
            h += (diff/tot)*room
        else:
            tot=s
            if tot<=1e-14: break
            h += (diff/tot)*h
    h=np.clip(h,0,1)
    s=h.sum(); diff=target-s
    if abs(diff)>1e-12:
       if diff>0:
          # add to largest rooms
          idx=np.argsort(1-h)
          for idx0 in idx:
             if s>=target: break
             add=min(1-h[idx0], target-s)
             h[idx0]+=add; s+=add
       else:
          idx=np.argsort(h)
          for idx0 in idx:
             if s<=target: break
             sub=min(h[idx0], s-target)
             h[idx0]-=sub; s-=sub
    return h
```
The final loop O(n log n) but only if diff >1e-12; rare. Good.
Potential `idx` argsort each final okay.

`resample_by_mass` project may change mass due to final exact sum; okay.

Now, **time check in run**: Use `if time.time() >= deadline: return best`. Ensure each phase not call helpers with deadline passed in past.

Let's draft run in more concrete:
```
def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    t0=time.time()
    deadline=t0+budget_s-5
    np.random.seed(seed)
    # parent
    if isinstance(globals().get('initial_h_values'), (list,np.ndarray)):
        h_parent = np.asarray(initial_h_values, dtype=float).copy()
    else:
        h_parent = default_h(500)
    n_parent = len(h_parent)
    h_parent = project_feasible(h_parent, n_parent)
    best_h = h_parent.copy()
    best_c = compute_c5(best_h, n_parent)
    best_n = n_parent
    # helper update function cannot closure; inline
    # Phase sweep
    sweep_n=[8,10,12,14,16,20,24,28,32,40,48,64,80,96,128,160,200,256,320,400,500,640,800,1000]
    sweep_end=min(t0+45, deadline-600) # if budget small adjust? use max
    if sweep_end < time.time()+2: sweep_end=time.time()+10
    for m in sweep_n:
       if time.time()>sweep_end: break
       if m>max(4,4*n_parent) and m>1500: continue? maybe not
       try:
          hn=resample_by_mass(h_parent,m)
          c=compute_c5(hn,m)
          if c<best_c:
             best_c=c; best_h=hn; best_n=m
       except Exception: pass
    # Low phase
    low_n=[8,10,12,14,16,20,24,28,32,40,48,64,80,96,128]
    low_end=min(deadline-300, t0+380)
    if low_end < time.time()+20: low_end=time.time()+20
    rem_count = len(low_n)
    for n in low_n:
       if time.time() >= low_end: break
       rem_count = max(1, len(low_n)-low_n.index(n)) # index O but small
       alloc = min(45.0, max(12.0, (low_end-time.time())/float(rem_count)))
       # if best already <0.3808 and n not? still
       cands=[]
       cands.append(resample_by_mass(h_parent,n))
       cands.append(rand_feasible(n))
       if n%2==0: cands.append(rand_binary(n))
       hb = cands[0]; cb=best_c
       sub_end = time.time() + alloc*0.55
       if n <= 64:
          max_ev = 40000 if n<=12 else 20000 if n<=16 else 10000 if n<=24 else 6000 if n<=32 else 3500 if n<=48 else 2000
          if sub_end > time.time()+5:
             hb,cb = de_lowdim(n, sub_end, cands, max_evals=max_ev, seed=seed+n)
       else:
          # quick continuous local from cands
          for cand in cands[:2]:
             cand=project_feasible(cand,n)
             c=compute_c5(cand,n)
             if sub_end > time.time()+3:
                cand,c2=lbfgs_optimize(cand,n,sub_end, configs=[(500,10),(2000,100)])? Maybe too.
             if c2<c: ...
             if c<cb: hb=c; cb=c
       # if DE candidate better update best
       if cb < best_c:
          best_c=cb; best_h=hb.copy(); best_n=n
       # binary hill even
       if n%2==0 and n<=128:
          bin_end = time.time() + min(alloc*0.35, 35.0)
          for start in range(2):
             if bin_end <= time.time()+2: break
             if start==0: binit = make_binary(resample_by_mass(h_parent,n), n)
             else: binit = rand_binary(n)
             max_ev=30000 if n<=32 else 15000 if n<=64 else 8000
             hb2,cb2=binary_hill_climb(binit,n,bin_end,max_evals=max_ev)
             if cb2 < best_c: update
             # optionally continuous from binary threshold? make_binary is binary; pair_descent may fractional
             if time.time() < low_end-2 and cb2 <= best_c + 0.002:
                hb2,cb2=pair_descent(hb2,n,time.time()+5,max_iter=10,max_pairs=80)
                if cb2 < best_c: update
       # continuous local on hb if n<=200
       if n<=200 and time.time() < low_end-5:
          local_end=time.time()+min(alloc*0.3,30)
          hb3,cb3=sqp_optimize(hb,n,local_end,max_iter=30,max_active=40)
          if cb3 < best_c: update
          if time.time()<local_end-3:
             hb3,cb3=pair_descent(hb3,n,local_end,max_iter=30,max_pairs=200)
             if cb3 < best_c: update
    # Parent/medium optimization
    med_end=deadline-60
    if n_parent <= 1200 and time.time() < med_end - 30:
       cand=h_parent.copy(); cn=n_parent; cc=compute_c5(cand,cn)
       avail=med_end-time.time()
       tsqp=time.time()+min(70, avail*0.4)
       if cn <= 1000:
          cand,cc=sqp_optimize(cand,cn,tsqp,max_iter=120 if cn<=500 else 70)
       if cc<best_c: update
       if time.time()<med_end-25:
          tlbf=time.time()+min(70, (med_end-time.time())*0.4)
          cand,cc=lbfgs_optimize(cand,cn,tlbf)
       if cc<best_c: update
       if time.time()<med_end-10:
          cand,cc=pair_descent(cand,cn,med_end-10,max_iter=40,max_pairs=200)
       if cc<best_c: update
    # Optimize current best if different or if not enough parent time
    if time.time() < deadline - 20 and best_n != n_parent and best_n <= 1500:
       cand=best_h.copy(); n=best_n; cc=best_c
       avail=deadline-20-time.time()
       # allocate based on n
       if n <= 250:
          if n <= 300:
             cand,cc=pair_descent(cand,n,time.time()+min(60,avail*0.3),max_iter=80,max_pairs=200)
             if cc<best: update
          if time.time()<deadline-25:
             cand,cc=sqp_optimize(cand,n,time.time()+min(40,avail*0.3),max_iter=100 if n<=200 else 50)
             if cc<best: update
          if time.time()<deadline-10:
             cand,cc=lbfgs_optimize(cand,n,time.time()+min(40, (deadline-time.time())*0.5))
             if cc<best: update
       elif n <= 1000:
          if n<=1000:
             cand,cc=sqp_optimize(cand,n,time.time()+min(50,avail*0.5),max_iter=60)
             if cc<best: update
          if time.time()<deadline-10:
             cand,cc=lbfgs_optimize(cand,n,time.time()+min(40,(deadline-time.time())*0.5), maxiter lower)
             if cc<best: update
          if time.time()<deadline-5:
             cand,cc=pair_descent(cand,n,deadline-5,max_iter=20,max_pairs=100)
             if cc<best: update
       else:
          cand,cc=sqp_optimize(cand,n,deadline-10,max_iter=30,max_active=30)
       # final SLSQP for small
       if len(cand) <= 200 and time.time() < deadline-10:
          cand,cc=slqp_polish(cand,len(cand),deadline-3,alpha=200000,maxiter=500)
          if cc<best: update
    # final polish if time
    if time.time() < deadline-8:
       n=best_n; cand=best_h.copy()
       if n <= 500:
          cand,cc=pair_descent(cand,n,deadline-3,max_iter=20,max_pairs=100)
       elif n <= 1200:
          cand,cc=sqp_optimize(cand,n,deadline-3,max_iter=20,max_active=20)
       if compute_c5(cand,n)<best_c: best...
    # ensure feasibility
    n=best_n; best_h=project_feasible(best_h,n); best_c=compute_c5(best_h,n)
    return best_h, float(best_c), int(n)
```

def make_binary(h,n):
    h=project_feasible(h,n)
    b=zeros
    if n%2==0:
       idx=np.argsort(-h)[:n//2]; b[idx]=1
    else:
       idx=np.argsort(-h)
       k=(n-1)//2
       b[idx[:k]]=1; b[idx[k]]=0.5
    return b
```

```
def pair_descent(h, n, deadline, max_iter=100, max_pairs=200, active_window=0.002):
    h = project_feasible(h, n)
    c = compute_c5_vec(h, n)
    best_h = h.copy(); best_c = float(c.max())
    no_improve = 0
    for _ in range(max_iter):
        if time.time() > deadline: break
        maxv = float(c.max())
        if maxv <= 0: break
        window = max(1e-9, active_window * maxv)
        act = np.where(c >= maxv - window)[0]
        if act.size == 0:
            act = np.array([int(np.argmax(c))])
        if act.size > 60:
            act = act[np.argsort(-c[act])[:60]]
        w = np.zeros(len(c), dtype=float)
        w[act] = 1.0
        w /= act.size
        g = weighted_grad_direct(w, h, n)
        interior = np.where((h > 1e-5) & (h < 1.0 - 1e-5))[0]
        if interior.size < 2:
            break
        # choose candidates
        order = np.argsort(g[interior])
        low = interior[order[:20]]
        high = interior[order[-20:]]
        cand = np.unique(np.concatenate((low, high)))
        Lcache = {}
        for p in cand:
            p=int(p)
            Lcache[p] = deriv_lag_vector(h, n, p)
        pairs = []
        for i in low:
            for j in high:
                i=int(i); j=int(j)
                if i != j:
                    pairs.append((i,j))
        # random pairs
        for _ in range(min(30, len(pairs))):
            a=int(interior[np.random.randint(interior.size)])
            b=int(interior[np.random.randint(interior.size)])
            if a!=b: pairs.append((a,b))
        # score pairs
        scored=[]
        for i,j in pairs:
            scored.append((abs(g[i]-g[j]), i, j))
        scored.sort(key=lambda x: x[0], reverse=True) # lambda? Is lambda a closure? It is a lambda, forbidden? "no closures or lambdas". Avoid lambda.
```
Need sort without lambda. Python list of tuples sorts by first element ascending; we can push negative score:
`scored.append((-abs(g[i]-g[j]), i, j))`; sort tuples default ascending (most negative first). No lambda. Good.

```
def pair_line_search(c_base, h, n, i, j, Li, Lj):
    tmin = -h[i]
    tmax = min(1.0 - h[i], h[j])
    if tmax - tmin < 1e-7:
        return 0.0, float(c_base.max())
    dx=2.0/n; mp=n-1+abs(i-j)
    diff = Li - Lj
    best_t = 0.0 if tmin <= 0.0 <= tmax else float(tmin)
    best_c = float(c_base.max())
    # Actually evaluate t=0 maybe
    best_c = pair_candidate_max(c_base,diff,mp,dx,0.0)
    # grid
    for stage in range(4):
       if stage==0:
          lo=tmin; hi=tmax; num=41
       else:
          step=(hi-lo)/(prev_num-1)
          lo=max(tmin, best_t - 2.0*step); hi=min(tmax, best_t + 2.0*step)
          num=81
       if hi-lo <= 1e-9: break
       ts=np.linspace(lo,hi,num)
       for t in ts:
          val=pair_candidate_max(...)
          if val<best_c: best_c=val; best_t=float(t)
       prev_num=num
    return best_t,best_c
```

Let's re-evaluate all gradient formulas using actual np.correlate.
We can either redefine f_k_actual(idx) = np.correlate value. Need gradient accordingly.
For idx m, k = m-(n-1). c_k_actual = ∑_{i} h_{i+k}(1-h_i) over i such that 0≤i<n and 0≤i+k<n? Let's set i is unshifted index (the 1-h argument). Then shifted index j=i+k. Conditions 0≤i<n,0≤j<n.
Derivative wrt h_p:
- If p appears as unshifted i: term with i=p if p+k valid: derivative (1 - h_{p+k})? Wait term h_{p+k}(1-h_p): derivative wrt h_p = -h_{p+k}. Not +(1-h). Sorry: term = h_{p+k} - h_{p+k}h_p. derivative = -h_{p+k}.
- If p appears as shifted j: i=p-k valid: term h_p(1-h_{p-k}), derivative = (1 - h_{p-k}).
So for k≥0:
  unshifted i=0..n-1-k: for p=0..n-1-k, grad[p] += -h[p+k]
  shifted j=k..n-1: p=p, i=p-k: grad[p] += 1-h[p-k]
Thus grad[:nv] += -h[k:n]; grad[k:] += (1-h[0:nv]) where nv=n-k.
For k<0, let kk=-k>0. c_{-kk}=∑_i h_{i-kk}(1-h_i), i=kk..n-1. Rewriting unshifted p=i, p=kk..n-1: derivative -h[p-kk]; shifted j=p, i=p+kk: p=0..n-1-kk: derivative 1-h[p+kk]. So grad[kk:] += -h[0:nv]; grad[0:nv] += 1-h[kk:] with nv=n-kk. This is exactly parent but signs/opposite? Parent for k>=0 had grad[0:nv]+= (1-h[k:n]); grad[k:n]+=-h[0:nv]. That matches actual for **negative** k? Let's compare parent k>=0 vs actual k<0 with kk? For actual k=-kk: grad[0:nv]+=1-h[kk:n]; grad[kk:n]+=-h[0:nv]. That's exactly parent's k>=0 with kk? Parent used h[k:n] and h[0:nv]. For positive parent uses 1-h[k:n] on low indices, actual negative uses 1-h[kk:n]. If k>=0, low indices; different index range. Actual single_shift_grad:
```
def single_shift_grad(h,n,idx):
    dx=2/n; k=idx-(n-1); grad=zeros
    if k>=0:
        nv=n-k
        # as unshifted low: -h[k:n]
        grad[0:nv] += (-h[k:n]) * dx
        # as shifted high: 1-h[0:nv]
        grad[k:n] += (1-h[0:nv]) * dx
    else:
        kk=-k; nv=n-kk
        # unshifted high: -h[0:nv] at idx kk:n
        grad[kk:n] += (-h[0:nv]) * dx
        # shifted low: 1-h[kk:n] at 0:nv
        grad[0:nv] += (1-h[kk:n]) * dx
    return grad
```
Check n=2,k=1: nv1; grad[0]+=-h1; grad[1]+=1-h0. Good.
k=-1: kk1; grad[1:2]+=-h0; grad[0:1]+=1-h1. c_-1=h0(1-h1): d h0=1-h1,d h1=-h0. Good.

Now, code snippets in detail:

```
def compute_c5_vec(h, n):
    dx = 2.0 / float(n)
    return np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode='full').astype(float) * dx
```
`astype(float)` maybe copy; not necessary. `np.correlate` returns float if h float. Use `float`? Good.

```
def compute_c5(h, n):
    return float(np.max(compute_c5_vec(h, n)))
```

Project:
```
def project_feasible(h, n):
    h = np.array(h, dtype=float, copy=True)
    h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
    target = 0.5 * float(n)
    for _ in range(30):
        s = float(h.sum())
        diff = target - s
        if abs(diff) <= 1e-13:
            break
        if diff > 0:
            room = 1.0 - h
            tot = float(room.sum())
            if tot <= 1e-15:
                break
            h = h + (diff / tot) * room
        else:
            tot = s
            if tot <= 1e-15:
                break
            h = h + (diff / tot) * h
    h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
    s = float(h.sum())
    diff = target - s
    if abs(diff) > 1e-12:
        if diff > 0:
            order = np.argsort(1.0 - h)
            for ii in order:
                if s >= target: break
                add = min(1.0 - h[ii], target - s)
                if add > 0:
                    h[ii] = h[ii] + add
                    s = s + add
        else:
            order = np.argsort(h)
            for ii in order:
                if s <= target: break
                sub = min(h[ii], s - target)
                if sub > 0:
                    h[ii] = h[ii] - sub
                    s = s - sub
    return h
```
Be careful `h = h + ...` creates new, okay. In final loops `ii` numpy int, h[ii]. Good.

Resample:
```
def resample_by_mass(h, m):
    n = len(h)
    if m <= 0: return np.zeros(0)
    if m == n:
        return project_feasible(h, n)
    dx = 2.0/float(n)
    xp = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n+1)
    cp = np.empty(n+1)
    cp[0]=0.0
    cp[1:]=np.cumsum(h)*dx
    # ensure monotonic/clip
    cp = np.clip(cp, 0.0, 1.0)
    # enforce total 1 at end
    cp[-1]=1.0
    # interp cumulative as nondecreasing? linear interpolation of nondecreasing is nondecreasing.
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, m+1)
    cnew = np.interp(x, xp, cp, left=0.0, right=1.0)
    mass = np.diff(cnew)
    mass = np.clip(mass, 0.0, 1.0)
    hnew = mass * (float(m) / 2.0)
    return project_feasible(hnew, m)
```
If cp clipped nondecreasing? h in [0,1] cumulative nondecreasing. Good.

Rand:
```
def rand_feasible(n):
    return project_feasible(np.random.rand(n), n)

def rand_binary(n):
    h = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    target = 0.5*float(n)
    idx = np.random.permutation(n)
    k = int(np.floor(target))
    if k > n: k=n
    h[idx[:k]] = 1.0
    rem = target - float(k)
    if rem > 1e-12 and k < n:
       h[idx[k]] = rem
    return h
```
For even rem 0. For odd rem 0.5. Good.

Default:
```
def default_h(n=500):
    return rand_binary(n)
```

Make binary:
```
def make_binary(h, n):
    h=project_feasible(h,n)
    b=np.zeros(n)
    idx=np.argsort(-h)
    target=0.5*float(n)
    k=int(np.floor(target))
    if k>n: k=n
    b[idx[:k]] = 1.0
    rem=target-float(k)
    if rem>1e-12 and k<n:
       b[idx[k]] = rem
    return b
```

Objective/grad actual:
```
def single_shift_grad(h, n, idx):
    dx=2.0/float(n)
    k=int(idx) - (n-1)
    grad=np.zeros(n)
    if k >= 0:
       nv=n-k
       if nv>0:
          grad[0:nv] += (-h[k:n]) * dx
          grad[k:n] += (1.0 - h[0:nv]) * dx
    else:
       kk=-k
       nv=n-kk
       if nv>0:
          grad[kk:n] += (-h[0:nv]) * dx
          grad[0:nv] += (1.0 - h[kk:n]) * dx
    return grad
```

`weighted_grad_direct` same but no allocate per lag? Use `wm` signed? weights positive.
```
def weighted_grad_direct(w,h,n):
    dx=2.0/float(n)
    grad=np.zeros(n)
    for m in range(len(w)):
       wm=float(w[m])
       if abs(wm) < 1e-12: continue
       k=m-(n-1)
       if k>=0:
          nv=n-k
          if nv>0:
             grad[0:nv] += wm*(-h[k:n])
             grad[k:n] += wm*(1.0 - h[0:nv])
       else:
          kk=-k
          nv=n-kk
          if nv>0:
             grad[kk:n] += wm*(-h[0:nv])
             grad[0:nv] += wm*(1.0 - h[kk:n])
    return grad*dx
```

`smooth_obj`:
```
def smooth_obj(x, n, alpha, mu):
    x = np.clip(x, 0.0, 1.0)
    c = compute_c5_vec(x, n)
    m = float(np.max(c))
    sh = alpha * (c - m)
    mx = float(np.max(sh))
    logsum = float(np.log(np.sum(np.exp(sh - mx))))
    val = m + (mx + logsum) / float(alpha)
    s = float(np.sum(x - 0.5*n)) # x sum - target
    val += mu * s * s
    return float(val)
```
`sum(x - target)` would be n*? Use `s = float(x.sum()) - 0.5*n`.

`smooth_grad`:
```
def smooth_grad(x,n,alpha,mu):
    x=np.clip(x,0.0,1.0)
    c=compute_c5_vec(x,n)
    m=float(np.max(c))
    sh=alpha*(c-m)
    mx=float(np.max(sh))
    w=np.exp(sh-mx)
    tot=float(w.sum())
    if tot <=0: w = np.zeros_like(c); w[int(np.argmax(c))]=1.0
    else: w = w/tot
    grad=weighted_grad_direct(w,x,n)
    s=float(x.sum()) - 0.5*n
    grad += (2.0*mu*s)*np.ones(n)
    return grad
```
Note for alpha large, w maybe [1,0]. Good.

`lbfgs_optimize`:
```
def lbfgs_optimize(h, n, deadline, configs=None, maxiter_override=None):
    h = project_feasible(h,n)
    best_h=h.copy(); best_c=compute_c5(h,n)
    if time.time() > deadline: return best_h,best_c
    bounds=[(0.0,1.0)]*n
    if configs is None:
       if n <= 64: configs = [(20.0,10.0),(100.0,100.0),(500.0,1000.0),(2000.0,10000.0)]
       elif n <= 256: configs = [(100.0,10.0),(500.0,100.0),(2000.0,1000.0),(8000.0,10000.0)]
       else: configs = [(500.0,10.0),(2000.0,100.0),(8000.0,1000.0)]
    for alpha,mu in configs:
       if time.time() > deadline - 2: break
       base_iter = 1200 if n <= 64 else 800 if n <= 256 else 500
       maxiter = base_iter
       if maxiter_override is not None:
          maxiter = int(maxiter_override)
       maxfun = max(50, maxiter*20)
       try:
          res = minimize(smooth_obj, h, jac=smooth_grad, args=(n,float(alpha),float(mu)),
                        method='L-BFGS-B', bounds=bounds,
                        options={'maxiter':maxiter,'ftol':1e-16,'gtol':1e-12,'maxfun':maxfun})
          h_new = project_feasible(res.x, n)
          c_new = compute_c5(h_new, n)
          if c_new < best_c - 1e-15:
             best_c = c_new; best_h = h_new.copy(); h = h_new
       except Exception:
          pass
    return best_h,best_c
```
At high n, mu e.g. 1000 may dominate; but equality. Good.

`slqp` top-level:
```
def slqp_obj(x,n,alpha): ... no penalty
def slqp_eq(x,n): return float(x.sum()-0.5*n)
def slqp_eq_jac(x,n): return np.ones(n)

def slqp_polish(h,n,deadline,alpha=100000.0,maxiter=500):
   h=project_feasible(h,n); best_c=compute_c5(h,n)
   if time.time() > deadline-5: return h,best_c
   bounds=[(0.0,1.0)]*n
   cons=[{'type':'eq','fun':slqp_eq,'jac':slqp_eq_jac,'args':(n,)}]
   try:
      res=minimize(slqp_obj,h,args=(n,float(alpha)),method='SLSQP',bounds=bounds,constraints=cons,options={'maxiter':int(maxiter),'ftol':1e-15})
      h_new=project_feasible(res.x,n); c_new=compute_c5(h_new,n)
      if c_new < best_c: return h_new,c_new
   except Exception: pass
   return h,best_c
```

`sqr?` SQP:
```
def sqp_optimize(h,n,deadline,max_iter=None,rho_init=0.05,max_active=None):
   h=project_feasible(h,n)
   best_h=h.copy(); best_c=compute_c5(h,n)
   if time.time()>deadline: return best_h,best_c
   if max_iter is None:
      if n <=80: max_iter=250
      elif n<=250: max_iter=140
      elif n<=600: max_iter=90
      else: max_iter=50
   if max_active is None:
      if n<=80: max_active=2*n-1
      elif n<=250: max_active=120
      elif n<=600: max_active=70
      else: max_active=40
   rho=float(rho_init); no_improve=0
   for it in range(int(max_iter)):
      if time.time()>deadline: break
      c=compute_c5_vec(h,n)
      maxv=float(np.max(c))
      tol=max(1e-10, 1e-4*maxv)
      active=np.where(c >= maxv - tol)[0]
      if active.size==0: active=np.array([int(np.argmax(c))])
      if active.size > max_active:
         active = active[np.argsort(-c[active])[:max_active]]
      A_ub=np.zeros((len(active), n+1))
      b_ub=np.zeros(len(active))
      row=0
      for idx in active:
         g=single_shift_grad(h,n,int(idx))
         A_ub[row,0:n]=g
         A_ub[row,n]=-1.0
         b_ub[row]=-float(c[int(idx)])
         row+=1
      A_eq=np.zeros((1,n+1))
      A_eq[0,0:n]=1.0
      b_eq=np.zeros(1)
      bounds=[]
      for i in range(n):
         lo=max(-rho, -float(h[i]))
         up=min(rho, 1.0-float(h[i]))
         if lo > up: lo=up=0.0
         bounds.append((lo,up))
      bounds.append((0.0, None))
      delta=None
      try:
         res=linprog(np.array([0.0]*(n)+[1.0]), A_ub=A_ub, b_ub=b_ub, A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=b_eq, bounds=bounds, method='highs')
         if res.success:
            delta=res.x[0:n]
      except Exception:
         delta=None
      if delta is None:
         rho*=0.5
         no_improve+=1
         if rho < 1e-8 or no_improve>20: break
         continue
      if float(np.max(np.abs(delta))) < 1e-13:
         break
      improved=False
      for factor in (1.0,0.5,0.25,0.125):
         h_try=project_feasible(h + factor*delta, n)
         c_try=compute_c5(h_try, n)
         if c_try < best_c - 1e-15:
            best_c=c_try; best_h=h_try.copy(); h=h_try; improved=True; break
      if improved:
         no_improve=0
         rho=min(0.15, rho*1.25)
      else:
         no_improve+=1
         rho*=0.5
         if rho<1e-9 or no_improve>25: break
   return best_h,best_c
```
`np.array([0.0]*n + [1.0])` list size n+1. For n=1000 ok. Could use `c_obj=np.zeros(n+1); c_obj[-1]=1`. Better.

LP objective: It may find delta that reduces linearized max but may violate inactive. Backtrack. Good.

`deriv_lag_vector` actual:
```
def deriv_lag_vector(h, n, p):
    L=np.zeros(2*n-1)
    start = n-1-p
    L[start:start+n] -= h
    L[p:p+n] += 1.0 - h[::-1]
    return L
```

`pair_candidate_max`:
```
def pair_candidate_max(base, diff, mp, dx, t):
    if t == 0.0:
       return float(np.max(base))
    vals = base + dx * t * diff
    vals[int(mp)] += dx * t * t
    return float(np.max(vals))
```
For performance, `np.max(vals)`.

`pair_line_search` with L arrays:
```
def pair_line_search(c_base, h, n, i, j, Li, Lj):
    tmin = -float(h[i])
    tmax = min(1.0 - float(h[i]), float(h[j]))
    if tmax - tmin < 1e-7:
       return 0.0, float(np.max(c_base))
    dx=2.0/float(n)
    mp=n-1+abs(i-j)
    diff = Li - Lj
    best_t = 0.0 if tmin <= 0.0 <= tmax else float(tmin)
    best_c = pair_candidate_max(c_base,diff,mp,dx,best_t)
    prev_num = 1
    lo = tmin; hi = tmax
    for stage in range(4):
       if hi - lo <= 1e-9:
          break
       if stage == 0:
          num = 41
       else:
          step = (lo0? need previous step)
```
Need track step from previous stage. Use variables:
```
    lo0=tmin; hi0=tmax; num0=41; step=(hi0-lo0)/(num0-1)
    best...
    # evaluate first grid
    ts=np.linspace(lo0,hi0,num0)
    for t in ts: ...
    step=(hi0-lo0)/(num0-1)
    for _ in range(3):
       lo=max(tmin, best_t - 2.0*step)
       hi=min(tmax, best_t + 2.0*step)
       if hi-lo <=1e-9: break
       num=81
       ts=np.linspace(lo,hi,num)
       for t in ts: ...
       step=(hi-lo)/(num-1)
```
Evaluate t=0 if inside: first grid includes maybe 0; but we also set best_t 0. Could just start best_c at max. Add if 0 inside: best_c = min(best_c, max). But grid includes endpoints not necessarily 0. Do:
```
if tmin <= 0 <= tmax:
   val0=float(np.max(c_base)); if val0<best_c: best_c=val0; best_t=0.0
else:
   best_t=float(tmin); best_c=pair_candidate_max(...tmin)
```
Then first grid includes endpoints and maybe not 0; fine.

At each t in ts, if `val < best_c`: update. This is deterministic.

Could use 3 refinement stages with 81. Good.

`pair_descent` with Lcache:
```
def pair_descent(h,n,deadline,max_iter=80,max_pairs=200,active_window=0.002):
    h=project_feasible(h,n)
    c=compute_c5_vec(h,n)
    best_h=h.copy(); best_c=float(np.max(c))
    no_improve=0
    for _ in range(int(max_iter)):
       if time.time()>deadline: break
       maxv=float(np.max(c))
       window=max(1e-9, float(active_window)*maxv)
       act=np.where(c >= maxv - window)[0]
       if act.size==0:
          act=np.array([int(np.argmax(c))])
       if act.size>60:
          act=act[np.argsort(-c[act])[:60]]
       w=np.zeros(len(c)); w[act]=1.0; w/=float(act.size)
       g=weighted_grad_direct(w,h,n)
       interior=np.where((h>1e-6)&(h<1.0-1e-6))[0]
       if interior.size<2: break
       order=np.argsort(g[interior])
       low=interior[order[:20]]
       high=interior[order[-20:]]
       # ensure high/low not same list issues
       cand=np.unique(np.concatenate((low, high)))
       Lcache={}
       for pp in cand:
          intp=int(pp)
          Lcache[intp]=deriv_lag_vector(h,n,intp)
       raw=[]
       for ii in low:
          for jj in high:
             i=int(ii); j=int(jj)
             if i!=j:
                raw.append((i,j))
       # random interior pairs
       rcount=min(30, max(1,int(interior.size)))
       for _r in range(rcount):
          a=int(interior[np.random.randint(int(interior.size))])
          b=int(interior[np.random.randint(int(interior.size))])
          if a!=b: raw.append((a,b))
       scored=[]
       for i,j in raw:
          if i==j: continue
          scored.append((-abs(float(g[i])-float(g[j])), i, j))
       scored.sort()
       if len(scored)>max_pairs: scored=scored[:int(max_pairs)]
       improved=False
       for negscore,i,j in scored:
          if time.time()>deadline: break
          if not Lcache: break
          Li=Lcache.get(i); Lj=Lcache.get(j)
          if Li is None or Lj is None: continue
          t,bc=pair_line_search(c,h,n,i,j,Li,Lj)
          if bc < maxv - 1e-13:
             old_h=h.copy()
             old_c=c.copy()
             h[i] += t; h[j] -= t
             h = project_feasible(h,n)
             c_new = compute_c5_vec(h,n)
             newc=float(np.max(c_new))
             if newc < best_c - 1e-14:
                best_c=newc; best_h=h; # h already new, but need copy?
                best_h = h.copy()
                c=c_new
                improved=True
                break
             else:
                h=old_h; c=old_c
       if improved:
          no_improve=0
       else:
          no_improve+=1
          # random kick with improvement? Try a few generic random transfers with line search maybe
          if no_improve > 8:
             break
          # perhaps do a random pair line search a few times even if gradient says no
          for _k in range(10):
             if time.time()>deadline: break
             a=int(interior[np.random.randint(int(interior.size))])
             b=int(interior[np.random.randint(int(interior.size))])
             if a==b: continue
             if a not in Lcache: Lcache[a]=deriv_lag_vector(h,n,a)
             if b not in Lcache: Lcache[b]=deriv_lag_vector(h,n,b)
             t,bc=pair_line_search(c,h,n,a,b,Lcache[a],Lcache[b])
             if bc < best_c - 1e-13:
                old_h=h.copy()
                h[a]+=t; h[b]-=t
                h=project_feasible(h,n)
                c_new=compute_c5_vec(h,n)
                newc=float(np.max(c_new))
                if newc < best_c - 1e-14:
                   best_c=newc; best_h=h.copy(); c=c_new; improved=True; break
                else:
                   h=old_h
          if improved:
             no_improve=0
    return best_h,float(best_c)
```
Issue: `h = project_feasible(h,n)` reassigns; `old_h` copy. Good.
`best_h = h.copy()` after break. In improved block before reassign? `h` local references old? We can simplify.
In loop `for negscore...`, after `h[i]+=t; h[j]-=t; h=project_feasible(h,n)`, if fail `h=old_h`. Good.
Random kick uses Lcache from previous h; if h not changed after random failed, Lcache valid. If improved, break.

Potential: `interior` computed before; if h changes in random kick then no recompute but loop next outer recomputes. Good.

`de_lowdim` uses `pair_descent` at end; for n small. Use `time.time() > deadline - 0.1`. Good.

`binary_hill_climb` actual c. Need use `np.random.randint(len(zeros))`, if zeros or ones empty? For even n, both size n/2. If h contains 0.5? make_binary even no 0.5. Good. But after swaps binary remains 0/1.
Should we use `compute_c5` each swap O(n^2). n up to128 and max_evals 8000, okay.

In `binary_hill_climb`, random kick accepts `c <= cur_c + 1e-12` (allows equal/worse tiny), but not worse to avoid. Good.

Now, **run** update best outside helper. We can't define nested `update`; just repeated code.

def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.time()
    deadline = t0 + float(budget_s) - 5.0
    np.random.seed(int(seed))
    # parent
    g = globals()
    if 'initial_h_values' in g and g['initial_h_values'] is not None:
        h_parent = np.asarray(g['initial_h_values'], dtype=float).copy()
    else:
        h_parent = default_h(500)
    n_parent = int(len(h_parent))
    if n_parent < 2:
        h_parent = default_h(500); n_parent=500
    h_parent = project_feasible(h_parent, n_parent)
    best_h = h_parent.copy()
    best_c = compute_c5(best_h, n_parent)
    best_n = n_parent

```
def pair_line_search(...):
    ...
    best_t = 0.0
    best_c = float(np.max(c_base))
    lo0=tmin; hi0=tmax; step=(hi0-lo0)/40.0
    ts=np.linspace(lo0,hi0,41)
    for t in ts:
       val=pair_candidate_max(...)
       if val < best_c:
          best_c=val; best_t=float(t)
    step=(hi0-lo0)/40.0
    for _ in range(3):
       if hi0? We need current lo/hi.
       lo=max(tmin, best_t - 2.0*step)
       hi=min(tmax, best_t + 2.0*step)
       if hi - lo <= 1e-9: break
       ts=np.linspace(lo,hi,81)
       for t in ts: ...
       step=(hi-lo)/80.0
    return best_t,best_c
```
